ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u267	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on the Government's draft law amending the Social Aid Act, No. 998 and No. 1001. On 10 October 2016, the Marshal of the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. The first reading and examination of the draft law was held on 15 October 2016 at a meeting of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs, which calls for its adoption by the High House. The draft law amending the Social Assistance Act provides for the amendment of the rule challenged by the Constitutional Tribunal by judgment of 6 October 2015, i.e. the granting of a permanent allowance from the moment of the application to the social assistance centre, together with the current disability decision or on the basis of decisions on total incapacity for work. In accordance with the amended Article. Article 106 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 7 in the case of a person who does not have a decision on total incapacity for work within the meaning of the provisions on pensions from the Social Insurance Fund or a decision on a significant or moderate degree of disability within the meaning of the provisions on occupational and social rehabilitation and employment of persons with disabilities, an application for permanent allowance together with the required documentation and confirmation of the application for a benefit dependent on incapacity for work or an application for a disability decision shall be initiated for the establishment of entitlement to permanent allowance and shall conduct a family environmental interview. Where the conditions referred to in Article are met. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 4, except for the condition of total incapacity for work, the proceedings shall be suspended until the date of delivery of the judgment. However, in the event of failure to comply with any of the conditions referred to in Article. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 4, of course, except for the condition of total incapacity for work, the grant of a permanent allowance shall be refused. Upon receipt of a decision on total incapacity for work or a decision on moderate or significant disability, the person applying for the benefit should file a decision at the social assistance centre within 60 days of receipt of it. The Centre will grant a permanent allowance from the month in which the application for a permanent allowance was made and there will therefore be a reversal of the period from which the permanent allowance will be granted. The submission of a decision after 60 days from the date of receipt will result in the grant of the benefit from the month in which the decision was delivered. During the period of waiting for the decision, the person will be entitled to an ex officio benefit without the need to apply for it. This requires that the statutory conditions for receiving this benefit, including those for income, are met. In this way, the person fulfilling the conditions for social assistance will not be deprived of the necessary means of subsistence. The proposed provisions will, on the one hand, allow the persons awaiting the judgment to receive appropriate assistance in the form of an interim allowance, on the other hand, allow them to obtain a permanent allowance for the period of waiting. The solutions adopted comply with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal and, as I said at the outset, the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy requests that this draft law be adopted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u269	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the government draft law amending the Social Assistance Act, print No. 998. The project entered the Sejm on 9 November. On 15 November, the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy adopted a proposal recommending that it be adopted by the High House. The draft constitutes the implementation of the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 6 October 2015. In that judgment, the Court held that Article. Article 106 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 3 of the Social Assistance Act in so far as it provides that, in the case of an application for a permanent allowance to a person who is completely unfit for work, legitimising a judgment of a significant degree of disability and not having other means of subsistence, the entitlement to the allowance shall be determined from the month in which the application was submitted. This Article is incompatible with Article. Article 67 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 in conjunction with Article. 2 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. In the context of the Court's challenge of the right to permanent benefit for persons who do not have a disability decision at the time of the application, the Government has decided to introduce a provision in the form of a periodic allowance whereby beneficiaries unable to carry out work will not be deprived of the source of subsistence until the decision has been obtained. This benefit will be paid ex officio without the need to apply for it. In addition, the legislator shall ensure that applicants are entitled to a permanent retroactive payment of the allowance as from the month in which they apply for the degree of disability, provided that the decision is delivered within 60 days of the date of receipt of the allowance. In order to avoid duplication of benefits, Article. Article 106 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. The 9th Act points to the need to determine the amount of the permanent benefit paid back on the basis of a previously granted interim allowance. The amendment also introduces the need to terminate proceedings which have been suspended for a long period of time, as well as transitional provisions allowing for the entry into force of a new legal act of persons who have applied for the benefit before the entry into force of the Act. It also guarantees the possibility to provide a disability decision within 60 days without consequences for the establishment of the right to permanent benefit. It therefore appears that the solutions adopted are optimally implementing the judgment of the Constitutional Court, providing financial security for people in difficulty during a period of time sometimes waiting to obtain a decision on the degree of disability or a decision on total incapacity for work. Therefore, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support the draft law proposed by the government. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u270	Thank you, Member. And please take the floor of Mrs Joanna Frydrych, the Civic Platform. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u271	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the government draft law amending the Social Assistance Act. In its judgment of 6 October 2015, the Constitutional Tribunal stated that it was incompatible with the Constitution of the Article. Article 106 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 3 of the Social Assistance Act, which provided that, in the case of an application for a permanent allowance to a person who is completely unfit for work, legitimised by a judgment of a significant degree of disability and without other means of subsistence, the entitlement to the allowance shall be determined from the month in which the application was submitted. The Court has rightly held that if a person has applied for a disability statement and subsequently for a disability which causes a lack of means of subsistence, he or she has applied for a permanent allowance, that benefit should be granted from the date on which he or she finds it impossible to take up work and not, as yet, from the time of the application. Draft Act amending the Social Assistance Act in Art. Article 106 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 9 assumes that, after receiving a decision on total incapacity for work or a decision on moderate or significant disability, the person applying for a permanent allowance should file a decision at the social assistance centre within 60 days of receipt of it. The Centre will then grant a permanent allowance from the month in which the application for permanent allowance was submitted. The above law introduces a solution which will allow the grant of a permanent pension to be granted backwards. Such an arrangement will ensure that they receive periodic allowance on a general basis to persons who are expected to receive a disability certificate and thus will not leave them without means of life. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Sejm's job is to make good law. In our view, the draft law on the amendment of the Social Assistance Act deserves support. The legal provisions contained in the proposed bill will contribute to ensuring that people unable to work and disabled are given adequate assistance in the form of a periodic allowance, as well as a permanent allowance for the period of waiting for a judgment, which will, in the long term, contribute to securing their basic needs in life. The legal framework should support weaker actors, and in accordance with Article. 67 The basic law of a non-work-free citizen who does not have other means of subsistence shall have the right to social security, the scope and form of which shall be determined by the law. The proposed changes will make access to benefits easier for citizens, and those who meet the conditions for social assistance will not be deprived of the necessary means of subsistence. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club states that the above amendments are correct and therefore supports the draft law amending the Social Assistance Act. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u272	Thank you very much, Member. And I ask the floor of Mrs Barbara Chrobak. You're welcome, my fellow Member.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u273	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law is a consequence of the need to implement the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 6 October 2015. It was this judgment that challenged the principle of granting permanent disability benefits only after the application had been submitted to the social assistance centre with complete documentation. The procedure for permanent allowance takes place in two stages - first, on the degree of disability, the district and voivodship teams appointed to settle such cases, and only in the second stage, in the form of an administrative decision, the right to benefit from social assistance is granted. In practice, this means that before a person with a disability receives an allowance, a very long period of time has passed. Often these are long months without the means to secure basic needs, not to mention over-standard needs, and these are the norm for people with disabilities. The State should bear this in mind and be particularly sensitive, I repeat, particularly sensitive to the needs of persons excluded from disability. At present, until a decision is taken, often people who should be assisted in a special way are left to themselves. So I ask: Where has the legislative power been so far, which allowed people affected by disability to remain without means of life during the period of waiting for a decision? I'm asking because the state should take care of these people in the first place. Let the amendment adopted today be the first step towards remedying the situation concerning people with disabilities. I would like to point out that the Constitutional Tribunal's judgments on compensation for the care of adults with disabilities are awaiting implementation. As a social movement, we will continue to look closely at the actions, but also at the omissions of the state in the area of care for people excluded from disability. The Kukiz Club15 will vote in favour of the project in its entirety. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u274	Thank you, Member. And Mrs Cornelia Wróblewska, please take the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u275	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Government's draft law amending the Social Assistance Act is the implementation of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 6 October 2015, which challenged the principle of granting permanent benefit only since the application was submitted to the Social Assistance Centre together with the current disability decision. The main objective of the project is to grant the social assistance allowance backwards, i.e. for the period prior to obtaining a disability decision or a decision on total incapacity for work. As mentioned at the beginning, under the current provisions, the benefit has been granted since the submission of the relevant documents at the social assistance centre - appropriate, i.e. including a disability ruling, and this is what is waiting for. From January 2017, it will be possible to obtain a pension back, i.e. from the time of the application for a benefit, even if the person does not have a decision on total incapacity for work within the meaning of the Social Insurance Fund pension rules or a decision on significant or moderate disability within the meaning of the occupational and social rehabilitation rules and the employment of persons with disabilities. The project also provides for the introduction of periodic allowance for persons awaiting a judgment. This is a fair solution, since these people are generally no longer able to work, and the State in granting assistance should be guided primarily by the concern for citizens, not by procedures that often take a very long time. This law gives hope to the government to act in favour of people with disabilities. But it's not the only judgment of the Constitutional Court that's waiting to be executed. Among those who are pressing are, among others. Judgments of the Constitutional Tribunal of 21 October 2014 and of 18 November 2014, the Constitutional Tribunal held in them that the inclusion in the Family Benefits Act of the age criterion of disability was a condition for obtaining care benefits, meaning that this criterion had to be removed from the Act. The modern draft amendment to the Family Benefits Act was submitted on 4 November this year. I ask the government to remove this project from the freezer and start the legislative process. I would remind you that the request to the Constitutional Court in this case, to consider the constitutionality of the age criterion for disability, has been filed with the Law and Justice. And finally, such a nice accent. Ladies and gentlemen, the next Saturday, December 3rd, is the International Day of Persons with Disabilities. It was established in 1992 by the United Nations General Assembly to draw attention to the problems of the most excluded social group and stress the need for action to integrate people with disabilities into the rest of society. On this occasion today in the Parliamentary Sejm, the Parliamentary Group on People with disabilities organized a special event for Members and Senators: a barrier-free parliament. Send, enter the shoes of a person with disabilities! From this point I cordially invite all your Members and Senators to the new House of Poselski, level -1, that is right next to the chapel. There are non-governmental organisations that will give you all the information about what it's like to be a disabled person. There are wheelchairs, there's an obstacle course. You're with the Vis Maior, which shows what it's like to lose sight, blindness, or not have any more eyes. You can see the guide dog. There is also a representative of Poland Without Barrier and a representative of the Polish Deaf Union. There are devices that allow, as I mentioned, to become familiar with what disability is. I cordially invite you to participate. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u276	Thank you very much. I would like to ask the floor of Mrs Joanna Augustynowska. You don't have an MP? This is Mr Krystian Jarubas, please.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u277	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party, I am pleased to present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Act on Social Assistance, print No. 998 and 1001. Ladies and gentlemen! The amendment introduces from 1 January 2017 the possibility of granting a permanent allowance for the period prior to obtaining a disability certificate. As a rule, the witnessing will be given backwards, but in accordance with rules. The benefits are now granted and paid from the month in which the application was submitted together with the required documentation - in this case the current disability decision. Only when the applicant lodges all the documents and receives the disability certificate shall the benefit procedure be initiated. However, these rules raised doubts about constitutionalists, and there was a fear that they were violating the rights of disabled people to help. Taking into account the fact that a permanent allowance should be granted to a person unable to work and in a difficult situation of income, the case was referred to the Constitutional Court. The Court's method of granting benefits to disabled persons with a significant degree of disability was dealt with in October. As a result of the judgment of the Court, the possibility of granting a permanent allowance was introduced for the period before the decision on the degree of disability was obtained. The application for a permanent allowance will need to be accompanied by a confirmation of the application for a disability decision. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u278	I would like to ask the floor of Mrs Margaret Zwierzan.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u279	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government proposal for a law amending the Social Assistance Act. The purpose of this bill is to implement the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 6 October 2015. The provision challenged by the Court refers to the determination of the right to a permanent allowance to a person who is completely unfit for work, legitimizing himself with a judgment of a significant degree of disability and who does not have other means of subsistence, starting from the month in which the person submitted the relevant application. The judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal provides for the relaxation of the existing provisions in the case of persons who are awaiting a disability decision or the granting of a benefit dependent on incapacity for work. The regulations under investigation introduce the possibility of receiving a permanent allowance before issuing a disability certificate for the applicant and a decision on the total incapacity for work. Applicants for the establishment of the right to permanent benefit will be able to submit appropriate applications in social assistance centres, accompanied only by confirmation of the application for an incapacity benefit or an application for a disability certificate. In addition, people who do not have the means to maintain during the period of waiting for a judgment will be able to receive a periodic allowance on a general basis. The changes contained in this draft not only follow the recommendations of the Constitutional Tribunal's judgment but also provide real assistance to those who, for a long period of time, expect a decision on the degree of disability or incapacity to work, often without the possibility of self-sustaining at that time. The Free and Solidarni Circle fully supports the bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u295	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to remind you that currently the lowest pension is PLN 882,56. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The method of valorizing pension benefits is one of the most sensitive and widely commented issues. In accordance with the current provisions of the Social Insurance Fund Pension Act, annual valorisation is carried out from 1 March using a percentage valorization rate, the main basis of which is the average annual price index of consumer goods and services in the previous calendar year, increased by at least 20% of the real increase in the average salary. All benefits paid are increased by the same percentage, therefore the lower the benefit, the lower the amount of the increase. In March 2016, the valorization of pension benefits resulting from the current legislation was embarrassingly low, because wages did not grow in Poland, and it must be admitted that there was also no special price increase. However, support for a wide group of pensioners and pensioners, while preserving the real value of all pensions and pensions, was the payment of a single allowance. The payment of this allowance resulted from the specially adopted on 15 January this year. Act on one-off allowance for certain pensioners and persons receiving pre-retirement benefits, pre-retirement benefits, bridge pensions or teacher compensation benefits in 2016. The payment of the allowance was complementary to the valorisation mechanism and its very low level, and the amount of the allowance depended on the amount of benefits charged, and it was PLN 50.400 net. The allowance provided support for a large group of recipients, because it was about 6.5 million people. The valorisation rate is also expected to be very low in 2017. This indicator is expected to be 100,73%. The government bill, which we are currently talking about, will therefore provide additional support for pensioners who receive the lowest benefits by increasing the lowest benefits to PLN 1000. This is an increase in the lowest pension by a dozen percent. In addition, the increase in the pension resulting from the valorisation must not be less than 10 PLN. That's the record in this bill. In this way all pensions and pensions will be raised. This principle will also apply to pensions for individual farmers, uniformed services, bridge pensions, teacher compensation benefits, veterans' pensions, war and military disability. Social pensions will be raised from PLN 741,35 to PLN 840, i.e. 98,65 zł. The lowest pensions, total invalidity pensions and survivor's pensions will increase from the current amount of PLN 882,56 to PLN 1000, i.e. 117.44 zł. As a result of the valorization alone, the forecast value ratio would increase to 889 PLN, i.e. Only at PLN 6.44. The lowest pensions for partial incapacity for work will increase from PLN 676,75 to PLN 750, i.e. about PLN 73.25. The adoption in 2017 of the principle of interest valorisation with a guaranteed minimum increase of PLN 10 will result in benefits paid under PLN 1369,86 per month being increased to a greater extent than if the current rules were to be valorised. approx. 2.1 million recipients will benefit from this valorisation principle. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u327	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! First of all, I would like to thank you for your vote in support of the government's draft law amending the Social Insurance Fund Pension Act. I also wanted to thank you for, I think, the lively discussion that I take as an expression. Indeed, the social importance, the social importance of this problem, which we are trying, seems to me to be effectively resolved by proposing to the High House today a draft law amending the Social Insurance Fund pension law. First of all, since this thread appeared in practically all statements, I would like to address the issue of the alleged exclusion of persons receiving benefits or receiving benefits calculated on the old rules, persons covered by the old pension scheme. It is not that this bill, which we propose, does not apply to persons born before 1 January 1949. I want to make this clear. [[Applause]] It is a project that concerns both persons covered by the so-called. an old pension scheme, as well as those covered by the new pension scheme, born after 31 December 1948. It is important to be aware of the differences, from the different assumptions on which the old pension system and the new pension system are based. In the old pension scheme, the achievement of an insurance period of 20 years for women and 25 years for men at that time was a condition for acquiring the right to a pension. Persons who reached the basic retirement age but did not achieve this required insurance period did not acquire pension rights in the old pension scheme at all, with one exception, namely the so-called. incomplete pensions, as laid down in Article. The 28th Pension Act, in which the provision of this traineeship, which was required for the acquisition of the right to a basic retirement age, was reduced by 5 years, but it acted as a condition for the acquisition of the right to a pension. It is worth remembering that in the new pension system, in the so-called. the defined contribution scheme, the pension period, the insurance period no longer constitutes a condition for the acquisition of the pension right. In other words, people who reach retirement age, according to today's legal situation, regardless of their internship situation, will be entitled to a pension, provided, of course, that they have paid at least one pension premium in the past. There is a group - and this is what asked me yesterday during the work in the committee of Mrs Augustynowska - one can say, in the old pension system, insured people who... Oh, one more thing I have to explain. In the new pension scheme, the achievement of an insurance, premium and non-contributory period of 22 years for women today, 25 years for men - according to the Act of 16 November 2016 reducing the retirement age, this pension will return to the period in force before 1 January 2013, in particular for women will be reduced to 20 years of today's 22 years - you can say, the insurance period, its achievement in the new pension scheme plays the role of covering the insured with the guarantee of at least the lowest pension benefit, as in the old system. In other words, whether the insured person was born before 1 January 1949 or was born after 31 December 1948 to obtain an insurance internship of essentially 20 years for women, 25 years for men decides to cover the insured person with the guarantee of receiving at least the lowest pension. Thus, those covered by the old pension scheme and those covered by the new pension scheme who do not fulfil this condition can say that they are not covered by the mechanisms that we propose in the law, i.e. above all, the mechanism for increasing the amount of the lowest pension benefit from PLN 882,56 to PLN 1000. In the old pension system, this will apply to 23 000. persons who are entitled to an under-retirement pension which is entitled to a reduced period of 15 years for women, 20 years for men after reaching the basic retirement age. On the other hand, as regards persons covered by the new pension scheme, according to our calculations, the number of persons who receive benefits below the amount of PLN 882,56 is 92 237. You also asked if you raised the problem of the construction of the lowest pension benefit in the defined premium scheme, where the amount of contributions paid during the insurance period, as well as the moment when the insured person decides to retire, decide on the amount of the pension benefit. So what I want to say is that this construction of the lowest pension provision in a defined contribution scheme, in which the right to a pension depends very much, can be said to be very important in the individual insurance past of the insured person, because it is one of the few social elements in this new pension scheme, which was introduced on 1 January 1999. This is also the case when it comes to the standard mechanisms used to value pension benefits. That is why we propose to refer these two solutions, which are contained in the bill discussed today, to those who receive the lowest pension benefits, because we see that the application of the percentage value of pension benefits leads to the separation of pension benefits from the minimum amount of social benefits. This minimum amount is currently around PLN 1000. We want this amount of the lowest pension benefit that we propose in the bill we are discussing today to be close to the minimum social amount. And one more thing, a thread that also appeared several times in the discussion. Namely, the problem, as far as these solutions we propose, are episodeal solutions, incidents, and as long as they are permanent solutions. So I would like to say that as regards the proposal to increase the amount of the lowest pension pension to, in principle, PLN 1000, with the exception of partial incapacity pensions, this is a permanent solution. This is a revision of the law, which is - it can be said - the statutory definition of the new amount of the lowest pension benefit with consequences in future years. As regards the issue of the modification of the standard mechanism for the valorisation of pension benefits, i.e. the addition of the quota element to the percentage valorisation, we are looking at a proposal for a one-off correction of the mechanism set out in the Social Insurance Fund Pension Act, but of course it is not that we are not considering any concept of systemic solutions in this area in the future. You know that we are currently as a government at the end of the pension review process. We have a statutory deadline by the end of December 2016 for the Council of Ministers to present a report on this review to the High House, and the issue of certain adjustments in the area of pension benefit valorisation is also the subject of this review. You also asked if the government was considering introducing mixed, percentage-quota valorisation. I would like to remind you that such a mixed, percentage-quota valorisation has been carried out twice in the past years, but I would also like to remind you that this type of valorisation mechanism has been assessed by the Constitutional Court. The Constitutional Tribunal conditionally, you can say, accepted this type of valorisation mechanism, but this condition means that it was supposed to be a one-off solution, that this condition is the one-time solution. So that we must remember that the systemic solutions we are thinking about must also take into account the constitutional requirements. Mrs Bartus also asked me about the issue of valorisation carried out in 2012, when the benefits were increased by 71 PLN, so I think it is worth referring to the element as if the costs of this operation - the valorisation we are proposing in the bill discussed today. So what I want to say is that, according to our estimates, the total cost of the two solutions we are proposing, i.e. the increase in the amount of the lowest pension benefits to PLN 1000 and the addition of the quota element to the percentage valorisation, will be about PLN 2.5 billion and the funds for this purpose are, of course, secured in the budget, in the draft budget. The draft budget assumes that at the standard percentage value of pension benefits at the level that you also asked me about, i.e. 0.73% 1,400 million zlotys were allocated. I would also like to say that this is an operation - I am talking about an increase in pension benefits by at least 0,73% - which will apply to all, including those whom you said would not benefit from a more favourable mechanism which we propose in the Act, i.e. those who are not covered by the guarantee of receiving at least the lowest pension benefit, as well as those who receive benefits at a level higher than PLN 1369,86 and will not therefore benefit from a pension increase of at least PLN 10. With regard to the costs of valorisation carried out in 2012, this cost amounted to PLN 7200 million, so this amount is several times higher than the costs of the solutions proposed in the Act. So, thanking you for the discussion, I would like to ask the High House to support the government's draft law amending the Pension and Pension Act of the Social Insurance Fund. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Thanks to the proposed regulations, shipbuilding operators and ship owners will have access to the production in Poland and meeting international standards of marine equipment. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u350	Thank you very much, Minister. The discussion is closed. We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 22 of the agenda: Report from the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the Government bill amending the law on the acquisition of property by foreigners and some other laws (prints Nos 992 and 1040). ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u351	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report by the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the Government bill amending the law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners and some other laws, print No. 992. Those other laws that are covered by this submission... The main part of the amendment concerns one of the oldest laws still in force in the Polish legal system, i.e. the law of 24 March 1920 on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners, but in this submission are also amended: the Act on Perpetual Books, the Act on the National Court Register and the Law on the Notariat. The amendment of this law is a consequence of the implementation of the audit recommendations of the Supreme Audit Chamber addressed to the Minister responsible for Home Affairs, formulated in the information on the results of the control of agricultural property sales of the Agricultural Property Resources of the Treasury by the field branches of the Agricultural Property Agency in Szczecin, Warsaw and Wrocław. This information results in recommendations indicating the need for the Minister responsible for home affairs to take action to ensure that the acquisition of full information on the shares or shares acquired or held by foreigners in companies owned or perpetually owned by agricultural property owners and to include in the kept register the real area of agricultural property owned or held in perpetual use by companies established in Poland in which foreign nationals acquired or acquired shares or held shares. At the outset, it should be stressed that the scope of the Act on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners concerns the acquisition of real estate exclusively on the territory of the Republic of Poland and the acquisition or acceptance of shares, shares or general rights and obligations only in companies established on the territory of the Republic of Poland which are the owners or perpetual users of real estate located on the territory of the Republic of Poland. In the current legal state, the records of the Minister responsible for Home Affairs referred to in Article. Article 8 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 The Law of 24 March 1920 on the acquisition of immovable property by foreigners is based on documents sent pursuant to Article. Article 8a (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 1 and 2, by notaries and courts. What are the documents? First of all, they are extracts of notarial acts by virtue of which the foreigner has acquired property or acquired or acquired shares or shares in a company owned or perpetually owned by the property owner, as well as final judgments of the court concerning the acquisition of real estate and the order of the court to include the acquisition or take up of shares in the register of economic operators. Therefore, notaries and courts are at present the only entities obliged to send the Minister responsible for internal affairs documents covering legal events to which the Law on the acquisition of immovable property by foreigners applies. The accuracy and timeliness of the information provided depends on the compatibility of the data entered in the above mentioned registers and presented in the annual report by the Minister responsible for Home Affairs on the implementation of the Law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners with the real level of interest among foreigners in real estate located in the territory of the Republic of Poland and their participation in the Polish capital market through the acquisition of shares or shares. From this inspection of the Supreme Audit Chamber, it appears that there have been a number of irregularities. From 2004 until the end of 2015, the Minister responsible for Home Affairs stated that 709 acquisitions of real estate, shares and shares were concluded in breach of the law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners and were therefore absolutely invalid on the basis of Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 Law of 24 March 1920. Of this number, in 476 cases, foreigners, after calling the authority, brought to a state compatible with the provisions of the Act, including 271 transactions involving foreigners from the European Economic Area. In the case of 233 transactions, the Minister responsible for. He was forced to apply to the general court for annulment of the acquisition of real estate, shares or shares. Therefore, the article is amended. 8. The following Article is inserted: Article 8a (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No The catalogues of the entities required to send the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs shall be extended. internal relevant documents, as well as those documents, shall be extended. In accordance with the proposed wording, notaries will be required to send an extract of the notarial act, an extract of the certificate of inheritance together with an extract of the inheritance protocol, contracts with notarially certified signatures and a copy of the European Certificate of Succession by virtue of which the foreigner acquired the property and by virtue of which the foreigner acquired or acquired shares, shares or general rights and obligations in a commercial company owned or perpetual user of the property located in the territory of the Republic of Poland. It should be noted that, in the current legal state, notaries are obliged to send only extracts of notarial acts, while drawing up e.g. the sale agreement of shares in a commercial company does not require that the form of a notarial act be maintained. On the other hand, the courts will be required to send copies of the final decisions on the basis of which the foreigner acquired the property, acquired or acquired shares, shares or general rights and obligations in a commercial company established in the territory of the Republic of Poland, as well as copies of documents submitted to the register, which show that the acquisition or acquisition of shares, shares or general rights and obligations in the company in the territory of the Republic of Poland which is the owner or perpetual user of the property has taken place and, where the acquisition or entry in the register of economic operators is subject to registration, also to a copy of the provisions issued on the subject. Taking into account the comments made by the National Notarial Council in Resolution IX/70/2016 of 9 September 2016, the applicants also proposed a new wording of the Article. Paragraph 7 2 and Article 2 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 1). Article 8 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 point 4 of the Law on the acquisition of real estate by The need for changes in the above mentioned the rules result from the changes in the law on succession which have taken place, and I will remind you, with the application of European Regulation No 650/2012 from 17 August 2015. At present, domestic law is no longer, in principle, the law applicable to succession, and the provisions of the law relating to succession should therefore be adapted. Added to the draft article. 5, which provides that the amendment of the Article. Paragraph 7 This Decision shall enter into force on the day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. On the other hand, as regards the amendment to the Law on perpetual books and mortgage, it is proposed to add the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs. internal to the directory of entities which may apply to the Minister of Justice, if necessary for the performance of statutory tasks, for consent to multiple, unlimited search of perpetual books in the Central Database of Perpetual Books. Obtained by the Minister responsible for Human Rights. internal access to the Central Data Base of Perpetual Books will allow for more efficient and more efficient control of the acquisition or acquisition of shares or shares by foreigners, because from the point of view of the authority, knowledge of real estate companies is crucial for assessing whether a given transaction is subject to the law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners. The next amendment proposed by the bill is to amend the Law of 14 February 1991. The draft law provides for the conclusion that the notary's failure to fulfil the obligation referred to in Article. Article 8a (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: The 1st Act is a disciplinary measure. The limitation period for this disciplinary measure of notaries from the current 3 to 5 years has been extended. The introduction of such a solution is supported by a number of shortcomings on an annual basis, which seriously undermine the integrity of the registers kept. It is enough to mention that only in 2011 and 2013 the then Ministry of the Interior revealed in the course of its investigations nearly 1300 cases of late transmission of notarial documents. Such a scale of the above problem causes that, given in the reports submitted to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland, the property purchased by foreigners can differ significantly from the actual state of affairs. It should be noted that delays in the transmission of notarial extracts can also often lead to a breach of legal certainty, as the Minister responsible for Affairs may question. the internal compatibility of the acquisition with the provisions of the Act will take place after a significant period of time, which also negatively affects the image of the State and the assessment of the activities of public administrations. It should be stressed here that in disciplinary proceedings concerning notaries who are late in sending notarial acts or who do not send them to the Minister of Home Affairs, there is such practice that these negligence so far in the case-law of notarial disciplinary courts is not regarded as disciplinary offences, but are regarded as reporting offences, since so far there have been no disciplinary sanctions in the Law on notaries that introduce only the current amendment. Despite the actions described, the Minister responsible for Home Affairs in 2014 and 2015 recorded in turn 449 and 318 cases, respectively, of failure to transfer notarial acts, including concerning the acquisition by foreigners of agricultural property from the Agricultural Property Agency, on the basis of the authorisation of the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. These acts included acts drawn up both before and after 2010. This indicates, firstly, that, despite the audits carried out, numerous notarial acts drawn up before 2010 have still not been sent to the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. This fact justifies the amendment of notarial law in this respect. In addition, it is proposed to introduce the obligation to include in the notarial act information on the nationality of a natural person, if he is a foreigner, and in the case of entities established in the territory of the Republic of Poland to specify or accept a declaration whether they are foreigners within the meaning of Article. 1 paragraph 2 point 4 of the Law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners, together with the justification. At the same time, this obligation will apply only to cases where a notarial act involves the acquisition of property by a foreigner or the acquisition or acquisition by him of shares, shares or general rights and obligations in a commercial company. In other cases, the notarial act will only contain the information that it has so far. From a practical point of view, given both the regulatory nature of the law on the acquisition of immovable property by foreigners and the information that should be introduced into the registers kept by the Minister responsible for Home Affairs, this change would bring several benefits. First, establishing the above information by a notary producing a notarial act would result in an assessment of the application to the contract of the law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners. Secondly, the notary's information about the status of foreigner held by the buyer would generate a direct obligation to send to the Minister responsible for internal affairs information about the transaction when the buyer was a foreign entity; if the buyer is a foreigner, the notary will be required to send the contract without having to examine whether the company in which the shares, shares or general rights and obligations are acquired or included is the owner or perpetual owner of the property. Another amendment proposed was the Act of 20 August 1997 on the National Court Register. The purpose of this amendment is to introduce an obligation for applicants to include in the application for entry or change of data in the register of KRS traders, which shows that there has been an acquisition or acquisition of shares, shares or general rights and obligations, or with the submission of registration documents pursuant to Article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 Acts containing information on changes of shareholders of the company statements, whether they are foreigners within the meaning of the Act on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners. These statements would be the primary source of information to the registry courts on the status of the entity concerned and would make it much easier to fulfil the obligation arising from the revised Article. Article 8a (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 2 Law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners. In the current state of law, the courts shall send only those provisions for which it is clear from the records that the shares or shares have been acquired or covered by the foreigner. In practice, this means that not all required court orders are sent to the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. The addition of the proposed provision will minimise this problem. The implementation of the obligations imposed by the project on entities included in the National Court Register should not consist in the submission of additional documents. Those amendments would enter into force on 1 June 2017 due to the need to adapt the electronic systems operating the National Court Register. In conclusion, the draft law on amending the law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners and some other laws is intended to improve the system of obtaining documents and information necessary to achieve the purpose set out above. Therefore, it is necessary to introduce new provisions in all the acts discussed above. At the same time, the proposed changes will not constitute a financial burden for the entities listed in point 4 of the impact assessment, nor for the State budget. In the opinion of the designer proposed in this draft law, the method guarantees the acquisition of the fullest knowledge of the property acquired by foreigners and the acquisition, acquisition of shares, shares and general rights and obligations in companies owned or perpetual users of real estate located in the territory of the Republic of Poland, as well as the elimination of infringements of the law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners, which will benefit the parties involved in legal transactions. The draft law shall not be notified in accordance with the procedure laid down in the Council of Ministers Regulation of 23 December 2002 on the functioning of the national system of notification of standards and legal acts. The project was included in the Public Information Bulletin on the website of the Ministry of Interior and Administration as required by Article. 5 of the Law of 7 July 2005 on lobbying activities in the legal process. No entity was interested in working on the project in this manner and therefore no comments were submitted. The draft law is not covered by European Union law, but according to the recommendation of the Development Team, the draft was agreed with the Minister responsible for the accession of the Republic of Poland to the European Union, in particular as regards freedom of establishment and freedom of movement of capital. In accordance with the position of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs of 5 February 2015, the collection of the data and information referred to in the draft normative act is permitted from the point of view of public policy. The Minister for Foreign Affairs has indicated that the bill is not contrary to European Union law. The first reading of the government submission took place at a joint meeting of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights and the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs on 16 November 2016. In principle, no substantive amendments were made to this proposal at the first meeting, but only amendments of a technical and legislative nature. The High Sejm announces the attached bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

In the assessment of the Supreme Audit Chamber, the rules currently in force are insufficient or insufficient to obtain complete information and documentation on such transactions, hence the proposal to introduce new solutions in this area and thus to improve the system of obtaining documents, among others. by extending the circle of entities obliged to inform the Minister of events subject to the law on the acquisition of immovable property by foreigners. The proposed amendments therefore concern, on the one hand, the extension of the catalogue of entities obliged to provide the Minister with relevant information, on the other hand, the extension of the catalogue of documents. Thus, as proposed by this amendment, notaries will be e.g. obliged to send the extract from the notarial act, the extract from the certificate of inheritance together with the extract from the inheritance protocol, the contract with notarially certified signatures and the European Certificate of Succession by virtue of which the foreigner acquired the property and by virtue of which he acquired or acquired shares, shares or general rights and obligations in the company in the territory of the Republic of Poland. Indeed, geopolitical, social and legislative conditions have changed over the 96 years of the law, which has not always been reflected in the present law. It is respectful here of the Supreme Audit Chamber's audit recommendations, which have brought to the attention of the High Chamber the need for urgent regulation arising from situations where, at times, gaps in its available part have caused or could often have had irreversible adverse effects on the Treasury. It is clear that, in order to ensure that the Minister's supervision is complete and appropriate, it is necessary to extend the catalogue of entities required to send the relevant documents to the Minister, as well as to extend the catalogue of those documents. It is appropriate to agree with the proposed provision that notaries should be required to send the discharge of the notarial act, the discharge of the certificate of inheritance together with the discharge of the inheritance protocol, a copy of the European Certificate of Succession, by virtue of which documents the foreigner purchased the property, took over the shares, shares or general rights and obligations in a commercial company owned or perpetual user of the property located in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The Minister responsible for Home Affairs is rightly added and the right to a multiple, unlimited search of perpetual books is imposed on the Central Database of Perpetual Books. It is reasonable to adjudicate in the Disciplinary Delicacy Act for notaries by extending its period from the current 3 to 5 years. It is appropriate to introduce the obligation to include in the notarial act additional information on the nationality of a natural person if he is a foreigner. Detailed provisions in the above matter will remove the problem of the notary's inability to determine whether the foreigner is a party to the sale of shares in the certificate of signature. The problem of entering into transactions with Polish companies in commercial law will be regulated. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u367	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the government proposal for a law amending the law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners and certain other laws. The regulations contained in this draft law are intended to help the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs. internal in the annual fulfilment of the obligations imposed on it by the law on the acquisition of immovable property by foreigners. According to this law, the Minister is obliged to keep a register of real estate, shares and shares acquired or covered by aliens without authorisation and with permission under the Act. According to the audit carried out by the Supreme Audit Chamber between 2011 and 2013, this register is not a reference by the current legislation, only on the basis of letters sent by notaries and by the courts, and given that not all of them fulfil this obligation, it is incomplete and incomplete. In order to enable the Minister responsible for. the internal proper keeping of the register, and thus its reliable compliance with the provisions of the law on the acquisition of immovable property by foreigners, the designers propose the introduction of a series of amendments to this law and certain other laws. As part of this amendment, they propose to extend the catalogue of entities required to send the relevant documents to the Minister, to extend the catalogue of these documents, to enable the Minister for Foreign Affairs. internal search of perpetual books in the central database of perpetual books. The authors of the amendment also propose to extend the limitation period of the disciplinary order of notaries from the current 3 to 5 years and to conclude that the notary's failure to send the relevant documents to the Minister's attention constitutes a disciplinary offence. In addition, it is envisaged that additional information on the nationality of a natural person should be included in the notarial act and, in the case of an entity established in the territory of the Republic of Poland, a statement on whether or not he is a foreigner is required. The changes introduced will result in the creation of a well-functioning system for obtaining relevant documents and information and will positively affect the reliability of notaries' information on transactions. This will improve the references of the register and thus contribute to the implementation of the audit recommendations made by the Supreme Audit Chamber. The Free and Solidarni Circle will support the procedated bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u377	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to the High House a report from the Extraordinary Commission on deregulation on the government draft law amending certain laws in order to improve the legal environment of entrepreneurs. The aim of the project is to remove a number of legal barriers from different branches of law that hinder or restrict the development of Polish entrepreneurship, create unnecessary red tape or significantly hinder the day-to-day activities of companies. In particular, the project provides for the introduction into the Polish legal order of the so-called. the legal certainty clause, ensuring that businesses are protected from changes in the interpretation of the law by public administrations, and provides for the introduction of important safeguards to protect the interests of traders in the course of the same control. It gives the possibility to lodge a complaint with the court for the duration of the control activities or the obligation to plan and organise checks based on a prior analysis of the likelihood of infringement, so that the checks are carried out not in arbitraryly selected undertakings, but in the first place where the likelihood of infringement is greatest. The solutions proposed in the project are particularly beneficial for micro, small and medium-sized companies. Savings for entrepreneurs and citizens resulting from the lower administrative burden reduced by this law will amount to about PLN 230 million, already in the first year of the Act. The bill quoted entered the Sejm on 9 November as a sejm print No. 994. On November 10, it was directed to first reading. It was held at its 30th meeting on 16 November in front of the High House. It was then decided by the High House that this draft should be addressed to the Extraordinary Committee on Deregulation in order to examine with a clear recommendation the submission of a report by 29 November. On 28 November, a committee meeting was held on which the draft law was presented. The project brings single amendments to 18 laws. During the discussion, the Legislative Bureau submitted a number of comments of a more precise, editorial and legislative nature. The amendments tabled were accepted by both the government and the members of the committee. During the meeting, Mr Jakub Kulesza, representing the Kukiz club15, proposed a package of amendments. Similarly, Mr Lenz, on behalf of the Civic Platform Club, proposed two amendments. The Parties to the amendments agreed with the President in the absence of opposition from the members of the committee to examine the amendments by the Government Party and the Legislative Bureau and refer them to the second reading. I would like to stress that the whole of the bill was adopted by the committee with no votes against. Therefore, I kindly ask the High House to adopt the report on the work of the committee in print 1064. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u422	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear three minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Anna April, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u423	Mr. Marshal! In fact, I am currently replacing Mr Marek Suski, who was to represent the applicants, but because the Amber Gold commission is currently working, unfortunately he could not present this resolution to us here. So let me refer to this project on the celebration of the 100th anniversary of the reactivation of municipal authorities in Radom, Kielce, Lublin and Piotrków. During World War I Poles made an effort to regain independence. They took the form of an armed act and diplomatic action in the international arena. However, it has proved important to build a civil society based on local government. It was not owned by the lands of the former Russian occupation, which were under German and Austrian occupation in the course of the war. In 1916 the occupied Austrian authorities issued a regulation reactivation of municipal authorities in Radom, Kielce, Lublin and Piotrków. By its power, on 7 December 1916 the first elections to city councils of these cities were held, after the period of Russian rule, in which their citizens decided about the composition of the magistrate. The elections held 100 years ago became an important element of the foundation on which a powerful building of self-government was soon built by the Reborn Republic. The efficient local government, based on the activity of all citizens regardless of nationality and religion, was one of the greatest successes of interwar Poland. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland - reaching also today to the best traditions of this self-government - wishes to celebrate the 100th anniversary of the reactivation of municipal authorities in Radom, Kielce, Lublin and Piotrków. I would like to stress that the elections held on 7 December 1916 contributed to the fact that 50 paradises were elected to the city council of Radom. At that time, the term of office of the council lasted for three years. This element of the construction of the civil state through municipal authorities probably had a significant impact on the functioning of the Second Republic. I just want to remind you that the city of Radom has been liberated. [[♪ The bell ♪]] On November 2, 1918, under the Austro-Hungarian occupation.

The current print project no. 1024 is as follows. It basically comes down to amending one paragraph - in Article. Article 33a (1) (a) (ii) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 4 - which is replaced by the following: In particularly justified cases, the Marshal of the Sejm, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, may: 1. extend the time limits referred to in paragraph. 1 and 3; 2. consent to the request by the Committee on Culture and Communications for additional, not more than two, projects referred to in paragraph. 1. Art. 2. The resolution shall enter into force on the date of its adoption. It is hereby recalled that in the original draft, the number of these resolutions was seven, and so far the rules in force in the Article. Article 33a (1) (a) (ii) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 3 is the provision that there may be five more resolutions during the year. This change is that there may be an additional two resolutions, but they are conditional, and that is already up to the marshal's empire, which, after consulting the Bureau, may introduce these two additional resolutions. He then agrees to the request by the Committee on Culture and Media to adopt two additional resolutions. I would like to remind you that these commemorative resolutions concern important events or people deserved in the history of Poland in various areas of life, political, scientific, cultural, social, economic. These commemorative resolutions have an increasing attendance, each year there are more of them than is actually allowed in the Rules of Procedure. Originally, only three such resolutions could be adopted for a year, then the limit was raised to five. Now five plus two, but these two are conditional. This is the essence of this regulation, of this incident, so to speak, changes to this regulation. On behalf of the committee, I ask the High House to support this draft resolution. Thank you. These three directives, High Izbo, should be implemented in our Polish legislation by the end of this year. The amendments introduced and proposed relate in particular, first, to the labelling, sealing and packaging of fruit plant propagating material, second, to the definition of seed and nursery material and, third, to the rules on the registration of suppliers and varieties. The current regulation of this issue by means of regulations of the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development is insufficient because it requires a great deal of commitment from the Ministry. The current regulation is inconsistent because cultivation of GMO plants is prohibited and the marketing of seed is not prohibited. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u476	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present a report to the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs on the Government's draft law amending certain laws on health and safety of the service, print no. 1049. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, sent the above draft law to the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs for first reading on 22 November 2016. The proposed law amending certain laws on health and safety in the service aims to bring the system of law into line with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 24 November 2015. At the request of the Ombudsman, the Court examined the constitutionality of the provisions of the laws which make provision for regulation in a lower order than the law on specific health and safety conditions of the service and acknowledged the applicant's right, in this case the Ombudsman. In the opinion of the Ombudsman, the contested provisions were contrary to Article. Article 66 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The second sentence of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, which explicitly states that the obligations of the employer and the way in which the right to safe and hygienic working conditions is exercised should be determined by law. Meanwhile, in WW. Laws do not specify the right or principles to safe and hygienic working conditions, in particular the obligations of the employer or the way in which the law is implemented have not been specified. In addition, the Ombudsman presented doubts regarding the compatibility of certain provisions of the order of the Chief of Police with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland and the implementation of the law and with the requirements of correct legislation. With regard to services other than the Police and the Border Guard for which regulations have been issued under the above laws, the Ombudsman stressed that it is a condition for the legality of the regulation to be issued for the purpose of implementing the law. This condition implies, first of all, the need to standardise in regulations only cases which are governed by the law and which serve to achieve the same objectives as those set out in the provisions of the Act. The Marshal of the Sejm also agreed with these allegations and additionally supported this opinion by the Attorney General. The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, following its first reading, in which only legislative amendments were actually tabled - they were notified by the Legislative Office of the Sejm, no amendments were tabled by the members of the committee - and after considering this draft law at its meeting on 28 November 2016 it is proposed that the High Sejm adopt the attached draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u478	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I have the honour of presenting my position. The proposed law aims to align the system of law with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of the Republic of Poland of 24 November 2015, sygn. file K 18/14, who noticed this problem. The Court found that, among other things, there was a lack of consistency. Article 2 Paragraph 7 1 point 6 of the Police Act of 6 April 1990 [[OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. U. z 2016 poz. 1782]] with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, because it contains an authorisation to regulate safe and hygienic conditions of service by means of an implementing act. This is contrary to Article. Article 66 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland, which, by establishing the universal right to safe and hygienic working conditions, imposed on the legislator an obligation to determine by law the manner in which this right is exercised and the obligations of the employer in this regard. In particular, the provisions introduced should include: first, the designation of the superiors responsible for the health and safety of the service and the determination of how they are ensured in relation to subordinate officers, secondly, the imposition on officers of an obligation to comply with the rules and principles of health and safety of the service, thirdly, the establishment of a legal standard conferring upon police officers and officers similar to those held by the worker, the right to refrain from carrying out their duties where the service conditions do not comply with the safety and hygiene rules of the service and pose a direct risk to the health or life of the officer, and the determination of cases where that standard does not apply, fourthly, the determination of the extent to which the safety and health rules of the service set out in the Law of 26 June 1974 apply in the service respectively. U. z 2016 poz. 1666. The Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the Government's bill to amend certain laws on health and safety of the service. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u480	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Parliamentary Club the Civic Platform to the Government's draft law amending certain laws on health and safety of the service, submitted in print no. 1049. This is a very important project, which is the implementation of the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal into our legal order and a response to the right comments of the Ombudsman. It assumes the protection provided for by the Labour Code on health and safety at work. This is extremely important, among others. from the point of view of the ability of officers to assert their rights. Previously, regulations on the safety of the service and its hygiene were standardized in regulations. According to Art. Article 66 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland has the right to safe and hygienic working conditions, and the manner in which this right and obligations of the employer must be exercised must be laid down by law. It must not be limited to those in employment, but the rules of this rank should apply to all, including uniformed officers. The current state of law in this area deprives officers of the possibility to assert the right to safe and hygienic working conditions, which can only be done within the limits laid down in the Act under the Constitution. In practice, after the entry into force of this Act, the superiors in the service will be obliged to apply the Labour Code. They will be obliged, among others. organise workstations in accordance with applicable health and safety regulations. The change of law also includes when officers, like employees, can refrain from carrying out their duties. They must have such a right when the conditions of service do not comply with the rules or pose immediate risks to health or life. In the course of the committee's work, correct amendments were introduced, presented by the Legislative Bureau, mainly editorial and standardising terminology. Therefore, given the arguments put forward earlier, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club supports the Act submitted in print no. 1049. It is important to proceed quickly over the draft law, as the Constitutional Tribunal ruled in its judgment that the existing provisions had expired on 3 December 2016. Thank you for your attention. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-30-sejm-31-2.u484	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present on behalf of the Poselski Club a modern position on the government draft law amending certain laws on the safety and hygiene of the service from print no. 1049. The committee's report is contained in print No 1069. Ladies and gentlemen! This draft law concerns the adaptation of certain provisions to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 24 November 2015, sygn. Act K 18/14, in which the Court found that certain provisions on the regulation of safe and hygienic conditions of service were unconstitutional. In accordance with the operative part of the judgment, it was considered that certain provisions, inter alia. the Police Act, the Border Guard Act, the Internal Security Agency and the Intelligence Agency Act or the Military Counterwitness Service Act and the Military Intelligence Service are in conflict with the article. Article 66 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The second sentence of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, which indicates that the obligations of the employer and the way in which the right to safe and hygienic working conditions is exercised should be laid down in the Act. Meanwhile, in WW. the rights to safe and hygienic working conditions have not been defined, and in particular the obligations of the employer and the way in which the law is implemented have not been clarified, and the legislator has merely indicated the authority to issue other acts which regulated the above mentioned. area. In addition, the Ombudsman expressed doubts as to the compatibility of certain provisions of the order of the chief police officer with the constitution and enforcement of the law and the requirements of correct legislation. In accordance with the grounds set out by the applicants, the proposed law, in strictly implementing the operative part of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, takes into account the essential part of the requirements laid down by the Tribunal in the statement of reasons for that judgment, which together should comply with the provisions of the laws standardising the safety and hygiene of the service. Turning to details, it should be noted, inter alia, that in the provisions of the article. 71a-71c is defined as persons responsible for the safety, hygiene of the service in relation to subordinate servants and training. In the proposed provisions, Article. 75a-75c of the Border Guard Act strengthened the right of officers to protect their lives and health by ensuring safe and hygienic service conditions. In the designed article. 75a the bodies responsible for the health and safety of the service are identified in the individual agencies of the Border Guard, as well as those carrying out the employer's tasks as defined in Section Ten of the Labour Code. In turn, in the article. 75b. 2 A solution was adopted to make use of the rules commonly used in this area in Section 10 of the Labour Code. Art. 75c. 1 requires officers to undergo periodic or inspection tests. The information to be included in the referral has also been indicated and the possibility of appeal against the medical certificate has been regulated, indicating the persons to whom the appeal is made, and an inspection body has been introduced. As above, in the case of ABW, AW, CBA, SKW and SWW, the rules clearly define the persons responsible for providing the officers with safe and hygienic service conditions and recall the regulations of Section 10 of the Labour Code, excluding certain provisions which are regulated in the current legal system concerning officers. To the end, I would like to note the provision added in the various laws, in particular in the article. 52a, in Article 52a, 56a and Article. 11a. It allows service-specific officers to refrain from complying with health and safety rules if, in the course of carrying out their duties, in circumstances justified by a higher need, the officer determines that there is a likelihood of saving human health or life or reversing a threat to the security of the State. It's an obvious necessity. Ladies and gentlemen! In view of the fact that this draft law implements the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 24 November 2015, sygn. Act K 18/14, the Modern Posel Club will support it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

At this point, it is worth noting that, while there was a full catalogue of records for health and safety at work for civil servants, such as the Police, the Border Guard, the Internal Security Agency, the Intelligence Agency and the Central Anti-corruption Office, there was indeed no legal regulations, which often forced government authorities to apply their own and their own approved regulations. They were very different depending on the type of service. As the Constitutional Tribunal rightly pointed out, the contested laws do not even contain a framework for the regulation of matters which are covered by the article. Article 66 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland should be governed by the legislator. Therefore, with regard to both the Police and the Border Guard, it is not permissible to regulate by way of regulations the issues which should be regulated in the Act. It is only the Sejm that has the legitimacy to statutory regulation and to determine the content and scope of the right to safe and hygienic working conditions, but also the services. The persons responsible for the safety and hygiene of the service shall be the bodies of the organisational units in which the officers serve. With regard to police schools responsible for these issues, they are made chiefs of these schools or police training centres. With regard to the Police Act, the provisions of Section 10 of the Labour Code are invoked, excluding those already regulated in the current legal state concerning officers. Different regulations will also apply to accidents or diseases in connection with the service. The specifics of the police force prevent the interference and entry of the State Labour Inspection in matters relating to police officers' professional relations. The vows made by police officers play an indelicate role, which directly results in accepting greater risks. An important change is the obligation on police officers to undergo periodic or inspection tests in order to establish a health assessment and to establish their capacity to carry out tasks in the occupied post. The amendment of the Police Act provides for and determines types of training in the field of health and safety of the service. In the Border Guard Act, the legislator strengthens the right of officers to protect their lives and health, ensuring safe and hygienic service conditions. Also in this case, the jurisdiction of the State Labour Inspection is excluded. Similar to the case of the Police and Border Guard, the amended and added regulations clearly define the persons responsible for providing the ABW, AW, CBA, SKW and SWW with safe and hygienic service conditions.
